# Common Time (chirpers10): the engine board

What the engine has that Accretion's pin did not, and what this record asks of it. The full list of what chirp can do,
feature by feature with its pin, is Accretion's board (`compose/chirpers9/features.md`); `python3 tools/chirpers.py palette
chirpers10-t1` shows what the song uses beside what it has not tried. Every row is opt-in: its default is exact, and each
pin is gated identical over the 109 released songs and this one before the groove adopts it.

The groove's pin is in `tools/groove-pin`; render with that binary (`$(cat tools/groove-pin)`), never the Nix chirp.

| `drive` `shape: wavefold` (chirper-zero, bd-d50192) | the wavefolder at its own level: `fold`'s shape with no output scale, so its level holds as `drive` moves and a lane can sweep it. `fold` scales its output by what a full-scale input comes out at, which is nothing at every drive where the fold lands on a zero (up to +60 dB): it stays as it was, bit for bit, for the released songs that use it at a fixed drive. On a sweep of `drive` 0.05 to 0.7 the peak moves 0.8 dB on `wavefold`, 9.3 dB on `fold`. With `bias` the output can reach twice full scale for a moment before the DC blocker settles, as `fold`'s does | 9d179572 (his 20a0cec4, cherry-picked onto the cabinet's commit by chirp-eco; changed lines identical) | 9d179572 (14 Nov 00:22) | Plain Hunt v3 (track 3): a second bass over `ct-bass` through the second cycle, `bass-fuzz fx.0.shape=wavefold` (the library patch's `fold` changed to `wavefold` in the song's patch string), high-passed at 180 Hz: the teeth at 250 Hz to 4 kHz, the low end left to the bass under it |
| reverb `drip: 0..1` on `kind: spring` (chirper-zero, bd-7becc2) | a spring that chirps: each of the spring's three loops carries a chain of up to 120 allpasses stretched to a transition near 4.8 kHz (the standard parametric spring model), and what enters is kept under the transition, so the highs of every echo arrive after its lows: the drip. At `drip: 1`, by `tools/dispersion.py` on chirp-eco's build, 2.8 kHz arrives 3.6 ms and 4 kHz 8.4 ms after 500 Hz on the first sound (500 Hz-2 kHz within 1.5 ms) and the echo trains lag by 4 and 12 ms; `drip: 0.5` is half of it (+1.9 / +4.9). Unset or 0 is the plain spring (three short loops, every band together), bit for bit: a released song uses it. Refused on any other kind. A model, not a measured tank: one transition, no second high chirp, no modulation; its first sound comes 3 ms (the lows) to 11 ms (4 kHz) after the note, so `predelay` adds a tank's 30-40 ms. It reads 0.4 dB louder than the plain spring at the same mix and fills the gaps more: re-set the mix. One reverb at `drip: 1` costs about a tenth of a core in real time, four times the plain spring (8 s of audio on one thread: 1.00 s against 0.23) | 2331cbf4 (his e8830ba0, carried past another project's commit by the landing; the chirp tree identical to the gated one) | e8830ba0 (14 Nov 02:57) | Odd Sympathy v1 (track 2): the two clean guitars' spring. Common Time v38: the low-gain rig at 2:13 and 11:19 (drip 1, mix 0.2) |

Add a row when you need something the engine lacks: say exactly what and why, and tell chirp-eco.

## Who builds them (Harry, 13 Nov 09:27)

Asked "who should build the engine features the song has shown are missing?" (chirp-eco, wM, chirper-zero, or not
now), Harry chose: **"chirper-zero builds them"**. His words of 12 Nov still frame it: "add new ones if we need better
amp sim or DI features. All chirp-native of course."

How a feature gets from the composer to the groove (chirp-eco gates, lands and adopts; the composer never pushes to
tools main and never moves `tools/groove-pin`):

1. **A worktree of your own at tools main**, outside `/tmp` (it is aged at ten days):
   `git -C ~/tools fetch -q origin && git -C ~/tools worktree add -b chirp-zero-engine ~/.local/state/chirper-zero/engine origin/main`.
   Main's engine is the groove's pin, so you start from what is playing. Read the source there, never in an old checkout.
2. **One bead a feature, one feature a landing, small.** Opt-in, and its default exact: with the option unset every
   existing song renders bit for bit as before. Released albums are frozen; the gate checks all 109 of their songs.
3. **Build in a guarded unit** so the groove's guard can freeze it, with a target directory of your own:
   `systemd-run --user --collect --unit=guarded-zero-build-$(date +%H%M) -p CPUSchedulingPolicy=batch -p Nice=12 --setenv=PATH="$PATH" --setenv=HOME="$HOME" --working-directory=$HOME/.local/state/chirper-zero/engine/cli/chirp -- bash -c 'nix develop -c bash -c "CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=4 CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/tmp/lt/target-zero cargo build --release -p chirp-cli" > /tmp/lt/zero-build.log 2>&1; echo EXIT $? >> /tmp/lt/zero-build.log'`.
   The binary is `/tmp/lt/target-zero/release/chirp`; bench with it by path. CPU only.
4. **Before handing over**, in the same shell: `cargo test --workspace && cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`
   (the README's own line), a test for the feature beside the code, and a line for it in `cli/chirp/README.md`.
5. **Hand over by message to chirp-eco**: the commit hashes on your branch, the bead, what you measured, and a PROBE: a
   small song or patch string that uses the feature, which your build plans and the groove's pin refuses.
6. **chirp-eco then** builds it afresh, runs the probe both ways, runs the gate (109 released songs identical, and this
   record's song identical), lands it on tools main, puts the groove on the new pin at the song's wrap, and adds the row
   above. About an hour and a half from hand-over when nothing differs.
7. **The song may not use a new feature until chirp-eco says the groove is on the new pin**: the groove renders with
   `tools/groove-pin`, and a song the pin cannot plan stops the record playing. Bench it in a copy until then.

Candidates, in no order (which, whether and when are the composer's call; the song comes first): the physical palm mute's
low mids (bd-bc17f7), the AW diphthong (bd-3b52e9), a rotary that does not force a from-the-top render (bd-e11da9), and
whatever the amp or the DI next turns out to lack. The three README notes that were waiting (the `stutter` `chance` rule, the `chaos` `amount` windows and the curve
of an `auto` lane's falls) went into the engine's README with 0d15b47e.

## Learned in use (how the engine behaves; none of these is a fault)

Each cost a round to find. They go into the engine's README with the next landing; until then they are here.

| what | the rule | found |
|---|---|---|
| On an L-system harp with `at: 305b`, track gain automation for a cell 55 sixteenths into the section belongs at **13.75b**, not 318.75b. The latter planned without complaint but the two upper harps stayed at exactly their original levels. | Count from the track's start and verify it on a solo render. With local points the fast and middle harps fall by 59 and 47 dB in the middle cell (including hall tails); the low harp's signal does not change. | Extreme and Mean v8, chirper-one |
| `risset.down: true` on the quiet pole's hi-hat gives a counter-rhythm to the steepening descent. The five 4-bar cycles of the solo stay within 0.3 dB of one another, but at the existing tick's fader (22 dB) the whole descent gains 5.2 dB at 8–16 kHz in the mono mix. | Give the moving taps space in the straight cymbal part before turning them up. At 19 dB the eleven-record reading at 8–16 kHz goes from 3.0 dB under to level; the hat's strokes sit a median 10 dB below the stereo mix. Whether the perceived speed keeps falling is an ear question, not a spectral one. | False Summit v15, chirper-one |
| live playback ignores a song's `loudness` | set the level in the song (a master `gain`, or `autolevel`); a file render's level says nothing about the groove's | v1-v3 |
| the plan drops values equal to the engine's default | `chirp plan` and `tools/showcase.py` show a reverb with no `kind` (it is a hall) and an amp with no `model` (it is `modern`) | 12 Nov |
| an amp is saturated from `gain` 0.3 up (soft and hard strokes 5.8 dB apart at 0.05, 0.5 dB at 0.3, 0 at 0.5) | a different string, pick or pickup is only a different guitar near `gain` 0.13; above it they move the spectrum about 2 dB at most | v14 |
| a compressed bass (or a clipped drums bus) ignores velocity and track gain | ride it AFTER the compressor: an `auto` lane on the bus gain | v8, v21 |
| a reverb inside a bus chain at a high `mix` turns the dry down with it (it cross-fades) | put the room on a send beside the bus, not in its chain | v10 |
| the `rotary` costs 2.7 dB of level at `mix` 1 | make it up on the fader | v15 |
| a track's name seeds its `humanize` | a null test across two track names is invalid; keep the name and change the patch | v15 |
| `stutter` reads `chance` at the start of each 256-frame block (about 5 ms) and decides a slot at its first frame; a lane rises in a straight line between its points and falls on a curve (the row below) | raise the lane half a beat before the slot and bring it down half a beat before the next: one raised 5 ms early missed its slot and caught the next. `tapestop`'s `stop` is a threshold (0.5) and is not touchy | v21 |
| an `auto` lane is an envelope: between two points a RISE is a straight line, a FALL takes the envelope's `curve` (default 3, exponential; `plan.rs` `points_at`). A gain lane from 0 to -20 dB over eight beats reads -3.0 -8.8 -12.6 -15.3 -17.1 -18.3 -19.2 -19.8 at the beats' middles; with `"curve": 0` in the lane's dict, -1.2 -3.7 -6.2 ... -18.7, the line | every fade-out, duck-by-lane or sweep down drawn as two points is a quick drop and a plateau (half the fall in the first 21% of the time) unless the lane says `"curve": 0`; fade-ins are lines either way. `tools/lanes.py ID` lists every lane, what it drives and each fall that is not a line | v24 |
| `sympathetic` rings on after the playing stops (its `decay`), and ANY ring ahead of a saturated amp holds the amp at full level | no `sympathetic` on a high-gain rig that must stop (bd-42e146) | v19-v20 |
| a held note through a saturated amp has no decay: the string dies, the amp's output does not until the string is 30-40 dB down. An 8 s chord falls 1-2 dB through `ct-chug`, `ct-wall` or `m8`, 37 dB through the low-gain `ct-crunch`, 48 through `ct-clean` | a chord written to ring out sits flat and then cuts. A ring-out is a fader move after the amp (an `auto` lane on the bus), not a property of the note. `tools/holds.py ID` lists every long note that sits flat with a rest after it | v9, v23 |
| a `rotary` remembers its start (so do `radio`, `vinyl`, `cloud`, `crush`, `ring`, `vocoder`, `autolevel`, a `tape` with a deck, `lpc`, `waveset`, `pvoc`): a windowed render (`--from POS --preroll N`) renders that effect's bus and every track routed to it from the top, so the take is the same | one such effect on a big bus makes every late window cost most of a full render: a solo guitar track at 12:44 took 38 s with the rotary on the guitars' bus, 2-6 s without. Keep such an effect on a bus of its own | v15, found 13 Nov |
| the noise-burst pick (`soft` 0, the default) gives a muted low string no period: through a saturated amp it is a flat hiss, level to 1 dB from its start to the next note. `soft` 1 is the triangle of a released string, and gives the kink its period back: a pulse train at the string's period (23 ms on a low F), 6-7 dB deep at 100-250 Hz | on MUTED notes bind `soft` to `expr.pm`, and hold the tone with the amp's `tight` (80 to 220) and an eq after. It is a growl, not a faster attack: on the Bleed bench (Eb2) the rise reads 20.2 to 15.5 ms, on Common Time's low-F riff 16.8 to 28.3. Bench: tight 3.7 to 1.4 (record 1.2), grain 0.26 to 0.40 (0.41); riffpunch 67.0 to 86.6. On every note it is worse on open notes (The Faultless 69.1 to 43.5). Against: guitarbench's low-mid contrast 18 (record 27) | v25 |
| effect rates take tempo-synced periods in beats (`1.75b` is seven sixteenths), and a synced `tremolo` is at its LOUDEST at whole multiples of its period from the song's start; there is no phase option | it locks to anything that counts from bar 1 (five's guitar is loudest exactly on seven's ticks). A track LFO does the same with `phase: 0.25` on a sine | v26 |
| an `orbit` circle round the listener reads +-4.3 dB left minus right on a tone (plus the delay between the ears) and costs 0.2-1.0 dB of level, radius 1 to 4 m alike; K-weighted a bell lost 1.3 dB of presence (its darkening behind the head) | make the level up on the fader if the part must not recede | v26 |
| `chirp render --rate 96000` works, but the plucked string is not the same string at another rate: an 8 s chord at 96 kHz against 48 kHz has 11-15 dB MORE of its energy at 4-12 kHz with no amp at all (`ct-clean`; `ct-chug` with amp and cabinet off), and the rigs shift 1-5 dB a band; a rig with an `ir` cabinet also comes out 2.8 dB louder | a 96 kHz render is not a test for the amp's aliasing: it changes the string first. Compare amps on the same DI instead | 13 Nov (chirp-eco's bench) |
| the wavefolder's `drive` cannot be swept: `drive shape=fold` scales its output by 1 / abs(sin(pi k / 2)) with k = 1 + 19 drive + 80 drive^3, a pole at every even k (capped at +60 dB). Common Time's snare stood so from v10 to v40 (on `drums`, clipped at -13 dB, and sent at -1.5 dB to `oh`, the cymbals' room): alone, peak -5.1 dBFS with the send and -11.4 without, 3.8 LU quieter without, its tail the room's 0.7 s | `tools/sendpaths.py SONG ... Both songs' cymbals, hat, ride and toms still go to the room at +4.5 and -1.5 dB: meant for cymbals, unchecked for the toms. | `tools/stembal.py` MISREAD SHORT WINDOWS OF A SONG WHOSE BEAT SITS ON ITS FRAMES (found and mended 14 Nov 14:2x). It read the spectrum in frames of 186 ms that did not overlap, each faded in and out, so a hit at a frame's edge hardly counted and one at its middle counted 2.7 times. On records and on Common Time (126 bpm) it evens out: the eleven records' median moved under 0.1 dB, Common Time's eight windows 0.5 dB at worst. At 108 bpm a beat is 2.99 frames, so in Odd Sympathy every hit on the beat stayed at one place in the frame through a short window: its six windows read up to 2.6 dB wrong at 8-16 kHz and 1.7 at 4-8 kHz, and the snare alone read 12 dB under what it is. Odd Sympathy v13 against the records at 2-4 / 4-8 / 8-16 kHz, as read before -> as it is: in step +1.0 +1.0 +0.7 -> +0.7 +1.2 +1.4; a sixteenth apart -1.3 -1.8 -1.6 -> -0.9 -1.0 0.0; an eighth apart -0.4 -1.8 -0.6 -> +0.1 -0.1 +2.1; three sixteenths apart +1.2 +1.4 +4.0 -> +0.9 +0.4 +2.7; a beat apart +0.6 +1.2 -0.3 -> +0.4 +1.3 +0.6; against +0.8 +0.4 +1.3 -> +0.7 +0.3 +1.3 | mended: every sample is counted alike now (frames a quarter of a frame apart), in `stembal.py` and in `eqpred.py` / `keybench.py`, which had the same frames (keybench's figures on this board re-read within 0.4 dB). Every "against the records" figure read on a short window of Odd Sympathy before v14 is worth reading again (v14 was made from the true rows). COMMON TIME, every window of its mix read both ways (v44): windows of eight bars or more were within 0.3 dB (median worst band; 1% had a band over 1 dB), six bars 4%, four bars 10%, three bars 13%, two bars 34% (worst 3.7 dB); its named windows stand (the seven break 0.5 dB at worst, its three-bar opening 0.9 at 8-16 kHz, the landing chords 0.3, the approach 0.7, the slow chords 0.8). For any new band reader: frames that fade must overlap, or do not fade | Odd Sympathy v3 to v13 (chirp-eco's fault in eqpred and keybench; stembal's frames were not checked when it was promoted) |

READ ALONE AND THEN IN THE BAND, the same raw mix with and without them: alone the floor tom was one line at 87 Hz, a hump from 250 to 500 Hz and nothing over 1 kHz; in the band all it added on a chorus's pull was the hump, +1.2 dB where the mix already stands over the records; in a fill a stroke had fallen only 3.8 dB when the next one landed. In the toms' own patch: the box out at the head (`tilt` -2 -> -6); a STICK (the patch's burst of noise at each stroke 26 dB louder and 12 ms long, its 4 kHz peak +2 -> +8 dB); the two small toms ring shorter (decay 1.3 -> 0.55 s) and the floor tom keeps its ring. The stick (2-6 kHz in the first 15 ms) -18.1 -> -4.6 dB; a fill stroke has fallen 9.1 dB when the next lands; the mix 0.971 -> 0.919: small, and the same way as the other two reads. A SIDE GAIN: the china lost 2.1 dB at 2-6 kHz whenever the tom struck with it (the drums' bus clipping the two together); now 0.9. HIS CORRECTION (11:14): one song carries the stick field that hisses, not two: Slow Enough to Count; Close Enough v13 only shortened its toms. Slow Enough to Count v23 (track 14, chirper-three, INSTRUMENT: the lead, the tune's guitar, 139 notes; no note changes; 85.93 -> 85.52, kept on judgment): the tune is the voice; three reads, three cures: (1) ITS NOTES: the lead alone stood 5.6, 4.1 and 5.6 cents SHARP on three seeds, the high F 12.2, in a song whose every note is a ratio of the count: the plucked string's own sharpness (the engine's switch for it came in batch 64 and the lead never had it); with the switch -0.4, -1.4, -0.6; (2) its echo falls on the count after the change of gear; (3) a voice's shape and level: a body at 500 Hz-1 kHz, without the bite at 2-4 kHz. Extreme and Mean v31 (track 7, chirper-one, 3:15.9 to 3:32.1; one new track, the song's first lead guitar, thirteen notes; 98.36 -> 98.26, kept on judgment): the short cells sing: a lead guitar sings a slow tune over all four guitars, then the same shape a semitone lower as the ground falls. A successor tom wants a stick and a flatter head by default, and the ring as its per-song field. READ A DRUM IN THE BAND AS WELL AS ALONE: the raw mix with and without it says what it ADDS (here, only its box); and two drums struck together clip each other on the drums' bus. FOR THE LEADS' FORK: a plucked lead wants `tune_sounding` in its patch: Slow Enough to Count's stood 4 to 6 cents sharp (12 on its top note) with nobody having asked; any `pluck` lead written before batch 64 is in the same state (a place to measure on each song: `lead`, `tune`, `lead-tape`). ENGINE: batch 66 (bd-d64c83) is half way through its gate at 11:13: probes passed at 10:52 on two seeds; 53 of the 109 released songs identical so far | 20 Nov: tracks 5 v34, 7 v31, 14 v23, 15 v21 |
| ALL FIVE CONTROLS OF A CHUG USED IN ONE ROUND, IN THE GUIDE'S ORDER, ON SIX GUITARS (the first whole pass by someone who did not write the guide's lines); two rounds (20 Nov, 10:27 to 10:33); and bd-d64c83 handed over, in the gate. Common Measure v33 (track 8, chirper-two, PALETTE: the album's two staple guitars, `ct-twin` (16 songs) and `ct-wall-stop` (9 songs), re-voiced at the stroke as this song's own; all six rhythm guitars change sound; no note, no other track and no bus changes; 92.20 -> 90.59, kept on judgment): the chug holds. WHY, his words: Harry, 19 Nov: 'we have so much we could do re: improving our instruments, better guitars'. READ FIRST, each guitar alone (`riffpunch.py --strokes`): the low end died (+1.9 to +2.8 dB on the four twins, +4.6 and +5.2 on the walls; the records -2.2 to +0.3); the twins' attack was soft (punch 2.8 to 3.4; theirs 3.5 to 4.2); the walls' attack was faster than any record's (9 and 11 ms; theirs 13.5 to 23.5). WHAT, in the order `compose/README.md` gives for a chug, a guitar at a time, about fifty builds: the pick half as soft under the palm; the thump halved on the main pair, off on the doubles and the walls; the amp tighter (220 -> 150 Hz) and the choke longer on the doubles only; the walls' palm filter 3.4 -> 2 octaves; the chain's dynamics: the front compressor lets the pick through (attack 2 -> 15 ms, release 60 -> 200 ms) and the transient shaper after each guitar's eq. The low end's fall, mean +3.08 -> +0.78 dB; punch inside the records' range, one of ten -> six; the walls' rise 9.0, 11.0 -> 13.5, 14.5 ms. Close Enough v24 (track 16, chirper-alpha, 3:20.0 to 3:22.1; thirteen notes on one new track and one stroke of the finger cymbals; 94.54 -> 94.55): every overtone has a last swell: the handpan goes on from where the band stopped, into the last chord. (The Other Hand v28, chirper-one's palette round, has his own row below: the dragged brush as a pitched friction patch) | FOR THE RIG'S FORK: Common Measure v33 is the nearest thing yet to the fork's bench done by hand: one composer, six guitars of both families, every control in order, ten reads before and after. Its settings and chirper-zero's and chirper-one's agree on the main points (pick half as soft; thump off or halved; walls' filter at two octaves; the compressor's attack at 15 ms; the transient shaper), and differ where the song differs (the amp's tightness, the choke, the chest band). So the successor rigs have a core that four composers reached separately, and a short list of per-song fields. ENGINE: chirper-zero built bd-d64c83 within the hour (both gaps; his refinement: the roots change places near `reso` 1.19, not at 1, so 'more of the batter' alone would have been wrong between the two); branch tip bffc1e5d; my gate is running (unit chirp-q66): my own build, probes on two seeds both ways, clippy, tests, the 109 released songs and the 17 in progress. It CHANGES what `tune_sounding` renders on a drum whose `reso` is over 1.19 (no song has one) and adds an opt-in `tune_exact`. Nothing is live until the 'LANDED' row | 20 Nov: tracks 8 v33, 16 v24 |

THREE THINGS FOR EVERY SONG ON THIS KIT (his, in `sounds.md`): (1) the kit's snare, written A3, sounds 243.5 Hz and wanders 20 cents either way stroke to stroke; `pitch.mod.0.amount=0` and `pitch.mod.1.amount=0` stop the wander; `cavity` moves the note and leaves the second line where it is; (2) MOST OF THE SNARE IS THE OVERHEAD ROOM: without its send the stroke's first 150 ms lose 15 to 17 dB from 1.25 to 3.15 kHz, so the humps the snare shows beside the records are mostly the room's; (3) so give the snare a room of its own. `snarespec.py` 5.3-5.7 -> 3.1-4.6; the mix nearer the records in all six stretches. Slow Enough to Count v21 (track 14, chirper-zero, INSTRUMENT: the wall pair, 96 chords; no note changes; 85.85 -> 86.04): the wall comes out of its box: the last wall pair on the library IR is on the engine's cabinet (Harry, 17 Nov; seven songs now); `cabspec.py` alone 5.10 -> 2.40; the guitars' share of the mix's 500 Hz-1 kHz 36% -> 22%. NOT AGREEING, said in the note: the voice rows are level; nobody sings there, so he took the no-voice rows and gave both. THE FALL IS THE SCORER'S CUT, not a section that stopped returning: the printed likenesses hardly move but one sits on the edge of the scorer's band (a return counts from 0.25) and at two of its seven thresholds the recurrence term then scores 18.9 for 100. The album kit's snare has `reso` 1.5: with the switch its loudest line lands at 150.8 Hz for a written A3, 654 cents flat, with a near-equal line 21 cents flat. At `reso` 1.0 and 0.7 it holds, 11 and 20 cents flat. Every released use I can find has `reso` 1.0, so a mend for `reso` over 1 need not move them: the gate will say. Until then: leave the switch off this snare and tune by `kit.s.pitch` (in `compose/README.md`). FOR THE KIT'S FORK: a successor snare wants its pitch mods at 0, its written note meaning its sounding note, and a room of its own; and a kit can FOLLOW THE SONG'S GROUND: on Close Enough the toms (v13), the bass drum (v21) and the snare (v23) each step with it, and False Summit v33 does the same with its bass drum. THE SCORER'S RECURRENCE TERM HAS AN EDGE (Where It Was v30): a likeness moving 0.25 -> 0.24 cost four points on one seed and nothing on another; a second stated limit of that term beside the one of 06:13 (a return in another key or tempo); nothing in the scorer is changed without Harry's word. Read a fall in recurrence on three seeds, as chirper-one did. | THE SNARE'S BOX COMES OUT AT THE HEAD: ten modes, not twenty-four (the first cure of the snare that is all in the patch); one round (20 Nov, 00:14). In the Time Of v26 (track 9, chirper-zero, INSTRUMENT: the snare, all 93 strokes; 94.54 -> 94.55): the snare's box comes out at the head; his own correction to Extreme and Mean v26 an hour before, where he cut the box with an equaliser and wrote that the hump was the head's own modes, as if out of the patch's reach: 'It is not. The engine's drumhead rings 24 modes by default and has a field for how many. I had not tried it.' Each field alone against `snarespec.py`: `mode_count` 24 -> 10 takes 1 kHz from +8.1 to +4.1 and 1.25 kHz from +6.9 to +0.8; `slap` 1.2 -> 0.5 takes 2.5 kHz from +6.9 to +3.3 (the slap IS the crack). Kept: ten head modes, the drum a tone lower (A3 -> G3), a harder, shorter stroke on a damped head (contact 0.45, damp 0.9, the amp's decay 110 ms), slap 0.8, the kit's +5 dB at 200 Hz to -3 because the note itself is there now. The snare alone 5.25 -> 3.32; the note at 200 Hz -17.8 -> -0.1; punch 12.0 -> 13.5 (the records 13.8); the fall to -20 dB 79 -> 68 ms (72); the kit by part at 250-500 Hz +3.2 -> +1.7 | THE SNARE'S RECIPE FOR THE KIT'S SUCCESSOR IS NOW WHOLE AND IN THE PATCH: `mode_count` 10, pitch G3, `slap` 0.8, `damp` 0.9, a shorter amp decay, and the 200 Hz lift taken off because the drum's note is there. Six songs mended this snare by hand before the field that makes the box was tried: a yardstick by thirds of an octave (`snarespec.py`) found in one evening what two ratios had not in a week. No engine bead: the engine had the field. `compose/README.md` and the tool's own text carry it | 20 Nov: track 9 v26 |

So a `ct-bass` successor wants the sine's level as its per-song field (chirper-zero, and now this), and the first read of a bass round is the bass's lowest notes (now in `compose/README.md`). FOR THE KIT FAMILY (chirper-zero): the LEVEL of the metal matters more than its tone, and a stroke-by-stroke read against the mix takes ten minutes a song; on the modal plate `bright` is a STEP (0.78 to 1 read alike, 0.72 drops the top modes); and So Far's plate copied whole read WORSE on Take Up the Slack (1.79 against 0.70): do not copy a plate between songs unread. That last one is a warning for the fork itself: a family's patch is fitted on a bench and then READ in each song it moves into. | A BREAKDOWN IS THE RIFF TWO SIZES UP, LEFT OUT; AND A FILL IS THE SONG'S CYCLE IN MINIATURE: five rounds (19 Nov, 04:57 to 05:13). Extreme and Mean v20 (track 7, chirper-alpha, on "play with breakdowns"; 99.61, kept on judgment): the breakdowns; neither is written: the riff is a long cell of 3 (stroke, stroke, rest) and a short of 2; two sizes up the same riff is a long of 8 and a short of 5, and in a breakdown the guitars, the bass and the foot keep their small strokes exactly where they were but ONLY where that larger riff strikes; where it rests, they rest. No stroke is moved, added or changed. Slow Enough to Count v4 (track 14, chirper-one, whole song: the drummer; 98.99): the fill is the cycle; the song had no fill anywhere, and under its rule a fill needs no invention: the three drums of the chord (F every 3 sixteenths, C every 4, A flat every 5, meeting every 60) say the WHOLE cycle in half the time or a quarter of it and are still on their own notes; five fills, 330 tom strokes. So Far v6 (track 17, chirper-three, INSTRUMENT: the first pair of guitars; 90.88): the guitars get their body; read alone against the records' lone guitars they were 5 to 6 dB short at 141-282 Hz and 5 to 7 dB over at 400-800 Hz; the weight of the muted low strokes is the guitars' own now, at the sides, where it had been the bass's in the middle. Where It Was v18 (track 13, chirper-three, the mix, no note changed; 99.43, kept on judgment): the band, read part by part; it began with the same bare band as So Far (bass on a plain track, the library's kick, one cut on the drums bus) and its parts had never been read against the records. Take Up the Slack v6 (track 15, chirper-two, palette; 99.33, kept on judgment): the crew's feet are feet; the stamps that open and close the song are five boots on a deck, one designed instrument, in place of the metal kit's kick (with its 10 dB of click at 4.5 kHz) and floor tom | SELF-SIMILARITY DOES THE ARRANGING: a rhythm scaled up is a mask for itself (the breakdown), and scaled down is its own ornament (the fill); both come from the song's rule and neither adds a stroke that was not implied. "Play with breakdowns" was built with no new note. An instrument round on one song is a recipe for its neighbours: So Far's three part-by-part reads (bass v3, kit v4, guitars v6) became Where It Was v18 the same hour, by the composer who made the third. A sound that MEANS something in a song (boots, a rope, a squeezebox) is designed for it; the kit's staples are for the kit. Two things an instrument round reads FIRST (his v16 to v17, now in the round's brief): a new voice's HARMONICS before its loudness (a bowed waveguide on a body is nearly all fundamental until it is shelved), and the changed window against the records BEFORE `done`, not after. A tempo can glide like a pitch when the tempo IS a pitch difference (Close Enough: turn the reed and the band accelerates). A round stopped by another's lock is not lost if it is left whole with a README: chirper-two took chirper-one's build, switch and all, 23 minutes later. In the Time Of v19 (track 9, chirper-two, 2:35 to 2:40; 91.06): every time once more; the five seconds of seven, six, five... strokes to the beat now run the song backwards, each speed with its own band and guest | READ THE MIX, AND READ IT WITHOUT A VOICE. (1) The plain `stembal` table compares a window with a median of records that mostly have someone singing: a window where nobody sings reads about 2.3 dB under at 500 Hz-1 kHz and looks like a hollow in the guitars. Common Time carried that false lead for five versions (riff A: -2.2 dB plain, +0.1 against the records' passages without a voice). A plain read now ends by saying so, and prints `no voice - all` and `OURS - REFS, no voice` when the records' voice split is on disk (config 86a332481; old against new on one window: the same lines plus three). Handoffs of 18 Nov that name a hollow at 500 Hz-1 kHz from a plain read are suspect. (2) The records' STEMS do not sum to their mixes (guitar -19.2, bass -26.6, drums -26.7 dB sum to -17.9; the mix is -10.8), so a rig fitted to a record's lone guitar can be right alone and wrong in the mix: round 63's 760 Hz cut put the rig within 1.4 dB alone and left the mix 2.2 dB under. Keep a tone change when the MIX's read agrees (`--voice`); the `instrument` round's brief says so. (3) Where both yardsticks agreed, the captured cabinet files were the fault and the engine's own cabinet the mend; the same pair is still on the files in tracks 4, 7, 8, 9, 10, 14 and 17: each needs its own read first. An engine want from a new song: sung /h/ onsets do not carry ("hand" read 0 times in 16, "man" 9 of 9; bd-43d2e1, chirper-alpha, for chirper-zero under the rule of 13 Nov). chirp-eco's sweep of the three newest songs: all carry the album kit's snare send to the overheads round the drums clip, as the template has it (noted on track 14); the written velocity ramps are cymbal swells and one crescendo | 19 Nov: tracks 9 v19, 11 v15, 15 v1, 16 v1 and v2; track 1's trial |
| A CHORD IS A RHYTHM TOO FAST TO COUNT: Slow Enough to Count v1, and two rounds (19 Nov, 01:21 to 01:42). Slow Enough to Count v1 (track 14, chirper-three, "the song I wanted to make"; 97.17): one rule picks rhythms and notes at once: WHAT STRIKES EVERY p SIXTEENTHS PLAYS THE NOTE WHOSE PERIOD IS p. The tempo is in the key: at 122.7756 BPM a pulse every three sixteenths is F exactly six octaves down, so common time itself is a C (the beat, the backbeat and the bar are C in three octaves), the dotted values are F (the djent chug) and the fives are A flat: threes against the kit's four are the power chord, add the fives and the groove is F minor, and the three come round together every 60 sixteenths. So Far v2 (track 17, chirper-three on chirper-one's new song, 34 minutes after its v1; 3:43.2 to 4:10.9; 86.69, kept on judgment): the summit has its fall inside it; v1's note asked that 160 be the biggest thing in the song and measured it was the smallest of the loud stretches (1.9 dB under the long walks): four guitars now, a crash, and the bass moves under the chord. In the Time Of v18 (track 9, chirper-one, 2:10.0 to 2:22.9; 91.05, kept on judgment): seven, counted by hand; the tabla that counted the fives comes back in the lock and plays the riff | a tempo can be CHOSEN so that the song's note values are its pitches: fix the key, and the BPM follows from which subdivision is to be which note (here to four decimals), after which every polyrhythm is literally a chord slowed down. A song its composer "wanted to make" is in the draw from its first version like any other: So Far got its second hand in 34 minutes, on exactly what its first note asked for. chirp-eco's sweep of Slow Enough to Count: `velramp` and `modlint` clean; `sendpaths` lists the snare and toms sent at -1.5 dB to the overheads round the drums clip (the shape Common Time's snare had from v10 to v40): left as a note on the song for whoever draws it next; whether it is a fault is for the ear. The gate's in-progress count follows the song files (15 with a version) | 19 Nov: tracks 9 v18, 14 v1, 17 v2 |
| THE FOUR NEW SLOTS WERE DRAWN ONE EACH WITHIN TWENTY MINUTES, AND THE FIRST IS STANDING: So Far v1 (19 Nov, 01:08). So Far v1 (track 17, chirper-one, "the song I wanted to make"; 4:42, 130 BPM, F; 87.03): take a number, halve it if even, treble it and add one if odd, until you reach one; the song tries the first sixteen in order and plays each walk in full, nothing repeated or cut to fit. A number is three things: its LENGTH (n sixteenths), its NOTE (the n-th harmonic of the low F, so a halving falls an octave in half the time) and its RIFF (its odd part written in twos, a stroke for a one and a rest for a nought); every walk ends alike, sixteen, eight, four, two, one. The kit is outside the rule and keeps common time | one rule can set length, pitch and rhythm at once when each is read off the same number, and an unproved conjecture gives a form with no repeats that still always comes home. The draw after Harry's words: chirper-one took track 17 at 00:48, chirper-two 16 at 00:53, chirper-three 14 at 00:58, chirper-alpha 15 at 01:00; chirper-zero, fifth to tick, drew Common Time (four slots, five composers: told to Harry). chirp-eco's sweep of the new song (`velramp`, `sendpaths`, `modlint`): clean. The gate's in-progress count is 14. Whole suite after the changes of 00:43: 138 files, 986 tests passing | 19 Nov: track 17 v1 |

Against the eleven records at 250-500 Hz: opposite +3.4 -> +0.1 dB, thumb +2.6 -> -0.8, raised +4.2 / +3.4 -> +2.2 / +1.1, one hand +4.4 -> 0.0; at 60-120 Hz: opposite -2.1 -> -0.7. `--without-album-kit` regenerates v10 byte for byte. The three waiting cards were asked again on their keys with clips from v11 (each before/after's "before" is v11 with only that part put back); the steel card says in its context that the steel is 1.0 dB more of its own band now the kit has left it. Left alone: "turn over" still reads +4.8 dB, three quarters of it the steel, which is what its card asks about. Where It Was v12 (track 13, chirper-one, 1:11.9 to 1:25.6; 99.26, kept on judgment): the negative in pitch; the tune takes four of the scale's seven notes, and each gap of the negative now takes the note that lies between the two it steps across | a low-mid excess read against the records was not the guitars: part by part, it was the kit, on a song whose skeleton was written by a composer new to the record and never given the album's kit eq. Where each song's drums bus stands now (read from the song files): tracks 1 to 5 and 7 to 9 carry the album kit eq whole (400 Hz -6, 5.5 kHz +1, shelf 9 kHz -2, low-pass 14.25 kHz); track 6 on its `kit` bus with the shelf at -4.5; track 12 now, without the low-pass; track 11 has 400 Hz -5 and the shelf only; tracks 10 and 13 have their own lighter cut (390 Hz -4, low-pass near 14.8 kHz). Whether 10, 11 and 13 want the album's is their next reader's to judge against the records: mine to point at. A card asked again on its key replaces the old one: thirty-five wait, as before | 18 Nov: tracks 12 v11, 13 v12 |

| A NOTE'S `expr_path` DOES NOT INCLUDE ITS START (chirper-alpha's bench; read against the engine source), and six rounds (18 Nov, 15:45 to 16:08). The Other Hand v10 (track 12, chirper-alpha, 0:54.8 to 1:05.8; 97.25): the line both hands make; read from opposite ends the two guitars' ringing notes add up to a line neither plays, and the ribbon draws its top, pitch by sixteenth. Where It Was v10 (track 13, chirper-one, whole song; 100.0, kept on judgment): the sheet turned over; read from its other end the eleven-tick cell's four notes rise, F, A flat, C, E flat: the record's own hook, which this tune was written as the falling answer to. On Its Hinge v11 (track 11, chirper-two, 2:41 to 3:00; 97.45 -> 97.61): the hinge turns harmonic where it turns fastest; the bass's grounds from 0:20 and 1:30 come back under the full band at the last five changes of feet. In the Time Of v13 (track 9, chirper-three; 83.72, kept on judgment): pipes at one to the beat; each speed on the way up had a guest and the return to one had none: a pipe organ (`organ-pipe`, on no other song) on a new track. Plain Hunt v24 (track 3, chirper-three, 3:32 to 3:42; 98.54, kept on judgment): the ringers' time; where a row is ten sixteenths and the bar sixteen, for eight rows the drummer counts the ROW instead of the bar. Carrying Capacity v16 (track 6, chirper-alpha, 1:00.4 to 1:02.6; 92.61, kept on judgment): what the doubling moved; eight is four said twice and the rule changes only three accents the second time: a second pair of guitars holds three chords on just those | on a native note the value AT NOTE-ON is `expr` and `expr_path` holds the points AFTER it: with N points the engine reaches point k (from 0) at (k+1)/N of the way from note-on to release, linear between (`Voice::expr_at`, plan.rs; the README's "moves it from `a` through the rest, evenly spaced"). So for a pitch per tick on a note of T ticks: put tick 0's value in `expr` and write N = T points, point k = the value wanted at tick k+1. Writing T+1 points with the start repeated runs the path almost a tick late at the start and a quarter-tick late three strips in (his bench: ~/.local/state/chirp-eco/common-time/expr-path-bench/bench.py). This is the engine as designed, not a fault; the README will say it in so many words with the next engine commit. The span ends at RELEASE, not at the end of the tail. Thirty-five cards wait | 18 Nov: tracks 3 v24, 6 v16, 9 v13, 11 v11, 12 v10, 13 v10 |

| THE RECORD IS A POOL FROM HERE (Harry, 18 Nov: "climbing should not have owenrs"; "a new round should release for anyuone to improve"), and the first song stood up under it: In the Time Of v1 (track 9, 03:35, chirper-zero; Harry to him: "for now youll build ahead ina  enew song"). One seven-stroke guitar cell dealt out at one to seven strokes a quarter over a fixed 84: the record's first quintuplets and septuplets; at seven to the beat the cell is exactly one beat long and locks; the opening returns as chords.
